                       OUR SCOPE




@gomezgardenes
                 Compartmental models
  Aimed at capturing the global (population-level) dynamics
        from the microscopic contagion processes




@gomezgardenes
                Compartmental models
  Aimed at capturing the global (population-level) dynamics
        from the microscopic contagion processes

         Each individual can be in one of n states at time t




   S - Susceptible (Healty)   I - Infected (and infectious)     R - Recovered (immune/dead)
@gomezgardenes                     (From Petter Holme’s blog)
                Compartmental models
  The transitions (e.g. S → I ) are mediated by some rates:

 The nal impact of an SIR epidemic is given by the fraction
           of affected (Recovered) individuals



                              λ                                     μ



   S - Susceptible (Healty)       I - Infected (and infectious)     R - Recovered (immune/dead)
@gomezgardenes                         (From Petter Holme’s blog)
     fi
                        Some examples
  Assume that individuals in the same compartment have same state regarding the
  disease: infectiousness, recovery time…

          λ            γ             μ
    S            E             I           R       Chickenpox, measles, rubella


          λ            μ             Often the exposed class is ignored, so that the
    S            I            R      mathematical model is simpler.

          λ            Some infections are better modeled by SI. In this case the host
    S            I     becomes infectious very fast and remain infected until death.


          λ            μ            Other diseases are better described by SIS. Here
    S            I            S     the host once recovered is soon again susceptible.


          λ            μ            η
    S            I            R            S      Temporary immunity

@gomezgardenes
 ℛ0 : Basic Reproductive Number
  • Average number of secondary cases an average primary case produces in a
    totally susceptible population.
  •




  • Determines whether a disease can successfully invade or not:




                                        ℛ0 can be measured
                                        experimentally from data




@gomezgardenes
ℛ0 : Basic Reproductive Number
• Average number of secondary cases an average primary case produces in a
  totally susceptible population.
•




• Determines whether a disease can successfully invade or not:




                                      ℛ0 can be measured
                                      experimentally from data
                                      [even its evolution ℛ(t)]

               Drawbacks of HMF
  Problem 1:
   • Under the HMF framework we are solving the epidemics problem for an
     ensemble of networks whose common feature is the P(k)
   • What if we want to solve the problem for a particular network?




  Problem 2:
   • MF approaches are useful to assess the critical properties of epidemic models,
     but they do not give us information about the probability of individual nodes.
   • Usually one relies on Monte Carlo simulations for that.




@gomezgardenes

 ℛ0 : Basic Reproductive Number

                 ℛ0 = T C P       ×        ×


         Main limitations:

                 All societies are identical
                 Excess of effective mixing among agents
                 Identical individuals, no heterogeneities
                 Closed and independent population

         Main limitations:

                 All societies are identical
                 Excess of effective mixing among agents
                 Identical individuals, no heterogeneities
                 Closed and independent population

 Compartmental models again…
                     EPIDEMIC                    SOCIAL
          S           Healthy              Ignorant / Non-user

           I         Infectious                 Spreader

          R     Recovered / Immune                Sti er


       Social contagions introduce new ingredients:

            Transmission from sender (Spreader) to receiver
          (Ignorant) are strongly in uenced by their behaviour
                            and perceptions.
@gomezgardenes

                             fl
 Compartmental models again…
                    EPIDEMIC                     SOCIAL
          S           Healthy              Ignorant / Non-user

          I          Infectious                Spreader

          R    Recovered / Immune                Sti er

       Two main categories depending on what is affected by behaviour:

         • Recovery (Transition I    S/R): Rumour Spreading

         • Infection (Transition S   I): Complex contagions
